Potential improvements to previous Adam:
1. Work in log-domain with normalization to simplex.
2. Multi-start with diverse n lengths and parameterized initializations (power singular, polynomial root approximations, random noise).
3. Use exact active-set / soft-max with varying temperature, maybe increase topk over time.
4. Use L-BFGS with line search for faster convergence.
5. Use annealing/noise to escape.
6. Use sequence length exploration: 2048, 4096, 8192. Since score tends continuous as n large; target 1.502 maybe needs large n. But previous n=6962 got 1.519. Maybe more length 12288 could get lower with same shape. Need manage time.
7. Use final "peak shaving" via local coordinate descent on top peaks: For each peak k, reduce it by adjusting variables in pair sums. Could use exact projection: Given active peak and sum constraint, choose pair (i,j) to transfer mass to reduce all peaks? Complex.
8. Use continuous PDE-like iterative smoothing: a_{new,i} ∝ a_i / sqrt(peak_involving_i) etc. Multiplicative amplitude damping of peaks.

6. delta = obj_grad - mean_grad.

Let's design mirror_optimize function:
Inputs: p0 (array length n, nonneg, maybe zeros), time_end, rng, params, maybe tag.
- Preprocess: p = p0 copy, clip zeros to eps = max(1e-15, min positive /1e6?) If all zeros, uniform. Normalize sum=1.
- We may optionally fix support if parent has zeros: Instead of log epsilon, we can use linear mode with zero mask. But for brevity, log with small floor. To retain zeros, set floor = 1e-12 relative, and after optimization we can prune exact zeros if all small.
- u = log(p).
- Develop u to improve: We'll implement two update styles: mirror with Adam-like momentum on delta.
State: m, v for q (delta normalized) maybe.
Pseudo:
```
def mirror_optimize(p0, deadline, rng, beta_start, beta_end, lr_start, tag):
    n=p0.size
    p=clip(p0,0,inf)
    mn=p[p>0].min()
    eps = max(1e-15, mn*1e-3) if mn>0 else 1e-12/n
    p = np.maximum(p, eps)
    p = p/p.sum()
    u = np.log(p)
    m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); t=0; best=score; best_p=p.copy()
    beta=beta_start; lr=lr_start
    while time<deadline:
        progress = (now-start)/(deadline-start)
        beta = beta_start + (beta_end-beta_start)*progress**0.5 (or stepwise)
        lr = lr_start*(1-0.8*progress)+0.01
        c=fast_self_conv(p)
        M=c.max()
        score = 2*n*M
        if score<best: save
        # weights
        cc = c - M
        # avoid overflow exp(beta*cc) beta*cc >= -beta*M. If beta large, tail exp underflow fine. If beta*M huge, exp(-huge) may underflow; no problem. But if beta*M > 700, all tail zero, okay.
        # Use np.exp(np.maximum(beta*cc, -50)) maybe.
        z=beta*(c-M)
        z=np.where(z>-50, z, -50.0)  # avoids underflow cost? np.exp(-1000)=0, fine.
        w=np.exp(z)
        w /= w.sum()
        # gradient h = 2 corr(p,w)
        h = gradient_weights(p,w,n)
        obj_grad = 2*n*h  # derivative score surrogate
        q = obj_grad - np.dot(p,obj_grad)
        # normalize q scale for Step:
        # Use RMS: rms = sqrt(mean(q^2)+eps)
        rms = np.sqrt(np.mean(q*q)) + 1e-8
        zstep = q / max(rms, 1e-8)
        # Adam on zstep
        t+=1
        m = 0.9*m+0.1*zstep
        v = 0.999*v+0.001*zstep^2
        step = lr * (m/(sqrt(v)+1e-8))
        # limit
        step = np.clip(step, -5, 5)
        u = u - step
        # recenter
        u -= np.mean(u)
        # update p
        p = np.exp(u - u.max())
        p /= p.sum()
        # optional noise
```
Will this converge? q is dimensionless gradient; Adam step magnitude lr. With lr starting 0.2, large. Need maybe lr=0.05. Could adapt with score check: if score increases significantly, reduce lr? But Adam nonmonotonic.

if score_new < best_current? update else halve alpha
```

Let's code helper functions:
- normalize(p): p=np.asarray float; p=np.clip(p,0,1000); S=p.sum(); if S<=0 uniform; return p/S.
- internal_score(p): c=fast_self_conv(p); return 2*len(p)*max(c) (since sum=1). To match evaluator scale-invariant exactly. Need if p sum not exactly 1, use formula with S. We'll keep normalized.
- fast_self_conv(p): as before.
- gradient_weights(p,w): via FFT correlation.

   * v = w[::-1]
   * L = 1 << (3*n - 3).bit_length() maybe next power2. For n small ensure.
   * P = np.fft.rfft(p, L); Wv = np.fft.rfft(v, L); conv = np.fft.irfft(P*Wv, L)[:3*n-3]
   * h = 2.0 * conv[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]

   For tiny n, 3*n-3 maybe 3; bit_length okay. For n=1, special? n likely >1. Add n=1 return [2*p0*w0]? Not needed but robust.

Let's also implement a "peak-flattening coordinate move": After local optimization, perform support-weighted local search over the top few peaks. For a chosen peak k with value c_k > M_target, we can reduce coefficient by adjusting variables in its pair sum. The derivative of c_k wrt p_i is 2 p_{k-i}. To reduce c_k while keeping sum and not increasing others, we might lower p_i for pairs with high p. But lowering p_i also lowers other peaks. Mirror gradient does this.

Could use exact least-squares active constraint: At optimum, M=max c. We want increase sum? fixed. Suppose we target all c_k ≤ M. def target_anneal(p0, deadline, rng, tag):
    p = normalized p0
    n=p.size; L=2n-1; avg=1/L
    best=score(p); best_p=p.copy()
    lambdas = [0.2,0.4,0.6,0.8,0.9...]
    caps maybe current max * [1,0.9,0.8]
    # Start with low lambda to avoid negative roots; increase over time? Or random.
    while time:
       choose lambda = min(0.99, 0.2 + 0.8*progress + random)
       cap = best_M * (1 - 0.2*progress) maybe
       b=conv(p)
       bwork = np.clip(b,0,None)
       bwork = np.where(bwork>cap, cap, bwork)
       target = (1-lambda)*bwork + lambda*avg
       target = target/target.sum()
       a = positive_square_root(target, n)
       a = normalize(a)
       sc=score(a)
       if sc < best: update, p=a
       else: maybe keep mixture p_new = (1-t)p + t a? However normalize positive. Or just continue from p.
```

Implement positive_square_root(target, length, direction=0):
Inputs target array length 2L-1? For recurrence need n = (len(target)+1)//2. But target might be longer? Should be 2n-1. We'll ensure.
```
def positive_square_root(b, n):
    b = np.asarray(b,float)
    m = len(b)
    # adapt n
    if n != (m+1)//2: n=(m+1)//2
    out=np.zeros(n)
    if n==1: return [sqrt(max(b[0],0))]
    a0=np.sqrt(max(b[0],1e-300))
    if a0 < 1e-15:
       # find first positive
       idx=np.argmax(b[:n] > 0)
       if idx==0: a0=1e-8; b[0]=a0*a0
       else: shift? Simpler set a0=1e-8
    out[0]=a0
    t=np.zeros(2*n-1)
    t[0]=a0*a0
    two_a0=2*a0
    for k in range(1,n):
       s=t[k]
       val=(b[k]-s)/two_a0
       if val < 0: val=0.0  # or if val > some clip
       if val > 1000: val=1000
       out[k]=val
       if val != 0.0:
           hi = min(2*n-2, 2*k)
           if hi > k:
               # j from k+1 to hi
               # out[k]*out[1:(hi-k)+1]
               out[k+?]? Need values out[1:hi-k+1] known? For j=k+1 => j-k=1; j=hi => j-k <=k. So known out[1:hi-k+1]
               t[k+1:hi+1] += 2*val*out[1:hi-k+1]
               if 2*k <= 2*n-2:
                   t[2*k] -= val*val
    return out
```

Helper to resample/upsample p to N using log interpolation:
```
def resample_probability(p, N):
    if p.size == N: return p.copy()
    n0=p.size
    if n0 < 2 or N < 2:
       ...
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n0)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,N)
    # use log(p+eps)
    eps = 1e-15 * max(p.max(),1.0)/n0? 
    log_p = np.log(np.maximum(p, eps))
    new_log = np.interp(x_new, x_old, log_p)
    q = np.exp(new_log - new_log.max())
    q /= q.sum()
    return q
```
If p has zeros, eps. This creates positive. For downsample, interp works.

Fast convolution:
```
def fast_self_conv(a):
    n=len(a); M=2*n-1; L=1 << (M-1).bit_length(); A=np.fft.rfft(a,L); return np.fft.irfft(A*A,L)[:M]
```
For n=1, M=1, L=1? (M-1).bit_length()=0, 1<<0=1. rfft length1 okay.

Score normalized:
```
def score_from_array(p):
    S=p.sum()
    if S<=1e-300: return np.inf
    c=fast_self_conv(p)
    return 2*len(p)*float(np.max(c))/(S*S)
```
For p normalized sum1, this equals. For internal target, use maybe score_normalized (S=1) but use general.

Gradient weights:
```
def gradient_correlation(p, w):
    n=len(p); L=2*n-1
    if n==1: return np.array([2.0*p[0]*w[0]]) # or 2*w[?]*p?
    v=w[::-1]
    full_len=3*n-3
    L=1 << (full_len-1).bit_length()
    P=np.fft.rfft(p,L); W=np.fft.rfft(v,L)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(P*W,L)[:full_len]
    h=2.0*conv[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
    return h
```
Check n=2 full_len=3, L=4? (full_len-1).bit_length()=2.bit_length? full_len-1=2 bit_length2? 1<<2=4 yes. Slice conv[1:3][::-1] length2. Good.

Weights:
```
def softmax_weights(c, beta):
    cmax=np.max(c)
    z=beta*(c-cmax)
    # z min could be -inf? c finite. Use np.exp(np.maximum(z, -50.0))
    w=np.exp(np.maximum(z, -50.0))
    s=w.sum()
    if s <=0 or not finite: return np.ones_like(c)/len(c)
    return w/s
```
If beta=0, weights uniform.

Mirror optimize:
```
def mirror_optimize(p0, time_end, rng, beta0, beta1, lr0, tag="mirror", max_iter=None):
    p=np.maximum(p0,0.0).astype(float)
    p=normalize_positive(p) # handle zeros
    n=p.size
    best=score_from_array(p); best_p=p.copy()
    u=np.log(p)
    m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); t=0
    no_improve=0; last_best_iter=0
    start=time.monotonic()
    total_time = max(1.0, time_end-start)
    while time.monotonic() < time_end:
        now=time.monotonic(); progress=min(1.0,(now-start)/total_time)
        beta=beta0 + (beta1-beta0)*progress
        lr=lr0*(1.0-0.75*progress)+0.005
        c=fast_self_conv(p)
        S=p.sum(); score=2*n*c.max()/(S*S)
        if np.isfinite(score) and score < best:
             best=score; best_p=p.copy(); no_improve=0; last_best_iter=t
        else: no_improve +=1
        if no_improve > 800: ... restart/perturb
        w=softmax_weights(c,beta)
        h=gradient_correlation(p,w)
        gradp=2.0*n*h
        mean=np.dot(p,gradp)
        q=gradp - mean
        # normalize q
        rms=np.sqrt(np.mean(q*q))
        if rms < 1e-12: return best,best_p
        z=q/rms
        t+=1
        # Adam on z
        m=0.9*m+0.1*z; v=0.999*v+0.001*z*z
        step=lr * (m/(np.sqrt(v)+1e-8))
        step=np.clip(step, -3.0, 3.0)
        u = u - step
        # optionally if no_improve high add noise
        if no_improve > 400:
             u += 0.05*rng.standard_normal(n)
        u -= u.mean()
        p=np.exp(u - u.max()); p/=p.sum()
        p=np.clip(p,0,1) # sum adjust
    return best,best_p
```
Need ensure q tangent property: subtract weighted mean (p dot grad) yields ∑ p_i q_i=0. But in u step, we also center u mean zero, which adds constant not affecting p after softmax? Actually adding constant to u doesn't change p; subtract mean after update safe. The tangent condition ∑p q=0 ensures scale invariance removed; centering u equivalent? Not exactly but fine.
Potential issue: `gradp=2*n*h`. Since h already 2*corr, score derivative = 2n * h? Wait earlier h=2∑p_j w_{i+j}; derivative of weighted sum φ=∑w c is grad_phi_i=h_i. Derivative of surrogate score 2n φ =2n*h. Good. If we instead want derivative of logsumexp, factor beta missing but weights normalized, direction same. Good.
Step size: q normalized by rms to unit. Adam step ~lr. Starting lr maybe 0.1. Could be too aggressive; but we can include a simple safeguard: after update, if new score > best*(1+0.2) and no_improve? Not necessary. We save best.
Restart: If no_improve large, set u from best_p plus noise, reset m,v,t. This can escape.

But computing gradient_correlation each step uses FFT full_len 3n; for n=12k, L=65536; two FFTs (p and v) plus conv irfft. fast_self_conv uses one rfft? Actually rfft p length for c? Could compute p_fft once and reuse for both? In mirror loop, we compute c via fast_self_conv, which does rfft p length 2n. Then gradient does rfft p length 3n and w. Can't reuse due different length. We can optimize: For gradient, if using topk loop maybe avoid extra. But FFT 65536 maybe okay. Steps? If 5000 steps, 5000*3 FFTs of 65536 ~ maybe too slow (several minutes). Need estimate: numpy FFT 65536 C ~0.5 ms? Actually 65536 complex FFT maybe ~1ms per transform? 15k transforms ~15s. okay. For n=16k L=131072 ~3ms, 15000 steps ~45s. Fine.

But Python overhead and memory maybe. Good.

Potential bug: positive_square_root expects b length 2n-1. If n large, t array length 2n-1; slice updates t[k+1:hi+1] += 2*val*out[1:hi-k+1]. For val=0, skip to save. For k near n, hi up to 2n-2. out slice length hi-k ≤ n-1. Good.

Target anneal function:

def target_anneal(p0, time_end, rng, tag, lambda_max=0.95, cap_factor=0.0):
    p=normalize_positive(p0)
    n=p.size; L=2*n-1; avg=1.0/L
    best=score_from_array(p); best_p=p.copy(); best_M=...
    lam=0.05
    it=0
    total_time=time_end-start
    prog=0
    while time< time_end:
       # choose lam based on it and random; maybe anneal
       lam = min(lambda_max, 0.05 + 0.05*(it//?))? Simpler: lam = min(lambda_max, 0.05 + 0.9*progress + 0.1*rand)
       b=fast_self_conv(p) # p sum1
       cap = None
       if cap_factor>0: cap = (best_M if finite else b.max())*(1.0 - cap_factor*progress)
       if cap: bwork=np.minimum(b, cap) else bwork=b.copy()
       target=(1-lam)*bwork + lam*avg
       ss=target.sum(); if ss>0: target/=ss
       # left root
       qL=positive_square_root(target,n)
       qL=normalize_positive(qL)
       evaluate; if better update best and maybe current p=qL
       # right root
       qR=positive_square_root(target[::-1], n)
       qR=qR[::-1]; normalize; eval
       # sym blend of best and qL? maybe.
       it+=1
       # if no improve many, reset p=best_p
```
Need choose progress. `total_time` might be small if called with short time; avoid division zero.
If λ schedule by progress, at start lam small; as time passes larger. But if called for short, lam maybe ~0.05. Good.
Current p update: We can set p to the candidate (left or right) with best score among the current trio if its score < current_score*(1+0.05) else blend 10% with current. This keeps stability. Since we maintain best anyway.

Let's code details carefully.

`normalize_positive`:
```
def normalize_positive(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size==0: return np.array([1.0])
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    S=a.sum()
    if S <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(S):
        return np.full(a.size, 1.0/a.size)
    # floor for zeros if needed? Return exact zeros? For mirror log require positive. This generic can preserve zeros.
    return a/S
```
`ensure_positive`:
```
def ensure_positive(p, floor=0.0):
    p=np.asarray(p,float)
    p=np.clip(p,0,1000)
    S=p.sum()
    if S<=0: return uniform
    p=p/S
    pos=p[p>0]
    if pos.size==0: return uniform
    minpos=pos.min()
    eps=max(1e-15, minpos*1e-6)
    p=np.where(p>0,p,eps)
    p=np.clip(p,eps,1000)
    return p/p.sum()
```
But if using exact zeros in target root, okay. Mirror needs positive.

`score_array` general, if S<0.01? Internal score for p normalize sum1; if not, use formula. It may compute c (could have nan). If not finite return inf.

`make_power_series(n)`:
```
c=np.empty(n); c[0]=1.0
for i in range(1,n): c[i]=c[i-1]*(1.0 - 0.5/i) # i int; for i large, ratio close1. 
# This stable? c_i ~ i^{-1/2}. For i=1 ratio0.5; okay. For n large, no underflow? c_n ~1/√n ~0.01, fine.
return normalize_positive(c)
```
Could also use simple `1.0/np.sqrt(np.arange(1,n+1, dtype=float))`.

`resample_probability`:
Use linear interp of p or log p? Log may better for tails. But if p has zeros, log. We can use p directly if no zeros; for singular, log. Implement with log after adding eps.
```
def resample_probability(p,N):
    p=normalize_positive(p)
    n0=p.size
    if n0==N: return p
    if N<2: return np.array([1.0])
    x0=np.linspace(0,1,n0); x1=np.linspace(0,1,N)
    eps = 1e-15 / max(n0,1)
    logp=np.log(np.maximum(p,eps))
    lp=np.interp(x1,x0,logp)
    q=np.exp(lp - lp.max()); q/=q.sum()
    return q
```

`positive_square_root` optimization:
Need handle b length maybe 2n-1; if not, adjust n.
Use local variables to reduce overhead:
```
def positive_square_root(b, n):
    b=np.asarray(b,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    m=b.size
    if m != 2*n-1:
        n=(m+1)//2
    if n<=0: return np.array([1.0])
    out=np.zeros(n)
    b0=float(b[0])
    if b0 < 1e-12:
       # If first target too small, add a little to first few? We can find first positive in first n; but for nonzero polynomial with no leading coeff, sequence could start later. To keep length, set a0=sqrt(max(b0,1e-15)).
       b0 = max(b0, 1e-12)
    a0=np.sqrt(b0)
    out[0]=a0
    conv=np.zeros(2*n-1)
    conv[0]=a0*a0
    two=2.0*a0
    for k in range(1,n):
        s=conv[k]
        val=(float(b[k]) - s)/two
        if val < 0.0:
            val=0.0
        elif val > 1000.0:
            val=1000.0
        out[k]=val
        if val != 0.0:
            hi = 2*k
            limit = 2*n-2
            if hi > limit: hi=limit
            if hi > k:
                # t indices k+1 .. hi
                segment_len=hi-k
                # out[1:segment_len+1]
                conv[k+1:hi+1] += (2.0*val) * out[1:segment_len+1]
                if 2*k <= limit:
                    conv[2*k] -= val*val
    return out
```
Test recurrence for baseline target b from P: If p sum1, b0 maybe ~1/n? target[0] maybe small but >1e-12? For n=7000, p0~0.006, b0~3.6e-5 >1e-12. fine.
If target after blending, okay.

Pseudo target_anneal:
```
def target_anneal(p0, time_end, rng, lambda_max=0.95, tag="target"):
    p=ensure_positive(p0)  # preserve small positive
    n=p.size
    avg=1.0/(2*n-1)
    best=score_array(p); best_p=p.copy(); best_M=best/(2*n) if n>0 else inf
    # But p sum1; best_M=cmax.
    cs=best
    start=time.monotonic()
    dur=max(1e-6, time_end-start)
    it=0
    no_improve=0
    while time.monotonic() < time_end:
        progress=min(1.0,(time.monotonic()-start)/dur)
        lam = min(lambda_max, 0.05 + lambda_max*progress + 0.05*rng.random())
        b=fast_self_conv(p)
        if b.size != 2*n-1: break
        b=np.maximum(b,0.0)
        # cap optional after progress
        if progress > 0.5 and np.isfinite(best_M):
            cap=best_M * (1.0 - 0.05*progress)
            if cap < 0: cap=0
            bwork=np.minimum(b, cap)
        else:
            bwork=b
        target=(1.0-lam)*bwork + lam*avg
        ts=target.sum()
        if ts <= 0: break
        target/=ts
        # left
        qL=positive_square_root(target,n); qL=normalize_positive(qL)
        scL=score_array(qL)
        cand=qL; scand=scL
        # right
        qR=positive_square_root(target[::-1],n); qR=qR[::-1]; qR=normalize_positive(qR)
        scR=score_array(qR)
        if scR < scand:
            cand=qR; scand=scR
        # sym blend L/R maybe if both not too bad
        sym=0.5*(qL+qR); sym=normalize_positive(sym)
        scS=score_array(sym)
        if scS < scand:
            cand=sym; scand=scS
        # also blend with best? maybe current blend.
        if np.isfinite(scand):
            if scand < best:
                best=scand; best_p=cand.copy(); best_M=scand/(2*n)
                p=cand.copy(); cs=scand; no_improve=0
            elif scand < cs:
                # accept current
                p=cand.copy(); cs=scand
            else:
                # small blend to current best direction? maybe if cand score not too bad
                alpha=0.1
                pnew=normalize_positive((1-alpha)*p + alpha*cand)
                scnew=score_array(pnew)
                if scnew < cs:
                    p=pnew; cs=scnew
                else:
                    no_improve += 1
                    if no_improve > 20:
                        p=best_p.copy(); cs=best
                        no_improve=0
        it+=1
    return best,best_p
```
This may be expensive but okay. `score_array` recomputes FFT. We already compute b for target; we could compute score of q without full? Need conv q. It's fine.

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    start=time.monotonic()
    total_b=max(1.0,float(budget_s))
    deadline=start+total_b-8.0
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)

    best_p=None; best_score=np.inf

    # load parent
    parent_arr=None
    try:
       arr=np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
       arr=np.clip(arr,0,1000.0)
       if arr.size>0 and np.isfinite(arr).all():
           S=arr.sum()
           if S>0.01:
               p=arr/S
               sc=score_array(p)
               if np.isfinite(sc): best_p=p; best_score=sc; parent_arr=p
    except Exception: pass

    # If no parent, create baseline at 4096
    if best_p is None:
       n = 4096
       p=make_power_series(n)
       best_p=p; best_score=score_array(p)
    base_len = best_p.size
    print("initial best score %.6f len %d" % ...)

    # helper for considering? We'll inline update.
```

Post-processing cheap with FFT; n up to 16384: trying 20 variants = 20 FFTs, fine.
Implement at end before final:
```
def postprocess(p, deadline, rng):
   best=p; score=score_array(p)
   n=p.size
   # mixtures reverse
   for alpha in [0.3,0.5,0.7]:
      q=2? alpha*p+(1-alpha)*p[::-1]; normalize; sc
   # power transform
   eps=...; pq=np.power(p, gamma); normalize
   for gamma [0.7,0.85,1.1,1.3]
   # zero small
   for frac [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3]:
      q=p.copy(); thresh=np.quantile(q, frac); q[q<thresh]=0; normalize; sc

import numpy as np

def ensure_positive(a, floor_rel=1e-12, floor_abs=1e-15):
    a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
    if a.size==0: return np.array([1.0])
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    s=a.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
       return np.full(a.size,1.0/a.size)
    a/=s
    pos=a[a>0]
    if pos.size==0:
       return np.full(a.size,1.0/a.size)
    mn=pos.min()
    eps=max(floor_abs, floor_rel*mn)
    # But if many zeros, floor may increase sum significantly? okay
    a=np.where(a>0.0, a, eps)
    a=np.clip(a, eps, 1000.0)
    return a/a.sum()
```
If a has zeros many, eps based on min positive; if min positive very small, eps small. Good.

`score_array`:
```
def score_array(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.inf
    a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
    S=a.sum()
    if S <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(S): return np.inf
    c=fast_self_conv(a)
    if c.size==0: return np.inf
    M=float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(M): return np.inf
    return float(2.0*n*M/(S*S))
```
Fast_self_conv can handle all zeros? If a zero, S check first.

`fast_self_conv`:
Use `np.fft.rfft(a, size=fft_len)`. For large a with values small, okay. If n=1, M=1, size=1 (1 << 0). Good.

`softmax_weights`:
```
def softmax_weights(c, beta):
    c=np.asarray(c)
    if c.size==0: return c
    cmax=float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(cmax): return np.ones_like(c)/c.size
    z=beta*(c-cmax)
    # avoid underflow and potential exp of -inf: z = np.maximum(z, -50.0)
    with np.errstate(over='ignore', under='ignore'):
       w=np.exp(np.maximum(z, -50.0))
    s=w.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0:
       return np.ones_like(c)/c.size
    return w/s
```
`beta` could be 0 -> z=0 uniform.

`gradient_correlation` as above. Need ensure `w` finite and sum1. If n=1, return `np.array([2.0*p[0]*w[0]])`? Derivative c0=p0^2 => grad=2p0 w0 times score 2n outside. Good.

```
def gradient_correlation(p,w):
    n=p.size
    L=2*n-1
    if n==1:
       return np.array([2.0*float(p[0])*float(w[0])])
    v=w[::-1]
    full_len=3*n-3
    size=1 << (full_len-1).bit_length()
    P=np.fft.rfft(p,size)
    W=np.fft.rfft(v,size)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(P*W,size)[:full_len]
    h=2.0*conv[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
    return h
```
Check for n=2 size=4? full_len=3, full_len-1=2, bit_length=2, size=4. Good.
Potential negative tiny in h? okay.

`positive_square_root` as above. Need take `b` maybe view reversed; use copy? `b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64).ravel()`; if b is reversed target, okay. Set `b = np.where(b > 0, b, 0.0)`? If small negative, set 0; if negative large, could hide. Use `np.maximum(b,0.0)` but if b0 negative, set to 1e-12.
```
b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)
```
But if all zero, output uniform? In target, not.
After loop, return out. If sum=0, uniform? The caller normalizes. But target root of positive sum should not.

`resample_probability`:
```
def resample_probability(p, N):
   p=normalize_positive(p)
   n=p.size
   if N <= 0: return np.array([1.0])
   if n == N: return p.copy()
   x0=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
   x1=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,N)
   eps = 1e-15 / max(n,1)
   lp=np.log(np.maximum(p, eps))
   # Optionally smooth by linear interpolation; if n<N and p has zeros, okay.
   lp1=np.interp(x1,x0,lp)
   q=np.exp(lp1 - lp1.max()); q/=q.sum()
   return q
```
If n=1, linspace ok.

return normalize_positive(c)
```

Now mirror_optimize code avoiding lambdas:
```
def mirror_optimize(p0, time_end, rng, beta0=None, beta1=None, lr0=0.15, tag='mirror'):
    p=ensure_positive(p0)
    n=p.size
    if beta0 is None: beta0=max(10.0, n/5.0)
    if beta1 is None: beta1=max(100.0, 12.0*n)
    best=score_array(p); best_p=p.copy()
    if not finite: return best,best_p
    u=np.log(p)
    u -= np.mean(u)
    m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); t=0
    no=0
    start=time.monotonic(); dur=max(1e-6,time_end-start)
    it=0
    while time.monotonic()<time_end:
       progress=...
       beta=beta0+(beta1-beta0)*progress
       lr=lr0*(1.0-0.7*progress)+0.005
       c=fast_self_conv(p)
       S=p.sum(); score = 2*n*max(c)/S^2
       if score < best: best=score; best_p=p.copy(); no=0
       else: no+=1
       if no > 800:
           # restart from best with noise
           p=ensure_positive(best_p)
           # add multiplicative noise
           p = p*np.exp(0.1*rng.standard_normal(n))
           p=normalize_positive(p)
           u=np.log(p); u-=np.mean(u); m[:]=0; v[:]=0; t=0; no=0
       if time.monotonic() > time_end: break
       w=softmax_weights(c,beta)
       h=gradient_correlation(p,w)
       D=2.0*n*h
       meanD=float(np.dot(p,D))
       g=D-meanD
       rms=np.sqrt(np.mean(g*g)) + 1e-12
       z=g/rms
       t+=1
       m=0.9*m+0.1*z
       v=0.999*v+0.001*z*z
       step=lr*(m/(np.sqrt(v)+1e-8))
       step=np.clip(step,-2.0,2.0)
       u = u - step
       if no > 300 and (it % 5 == 0):
           u += 0.03*rng.standard_normal(n)
       u -= np.mean(u)
       p=np.exp(u-u.max()); p/=p.sum()
       p=np.clip(p,0,1); if p.sum() >0: p/=p.sum()
       it+=1
       if it%100==0: print(tag, it, best)
    return best,best_p
```
Potential memory: m,v arrays length n; okay.

Should we use exact max c and beta? Yes.

def postprocess_candidate(p, time_end, rng):
   p=normalize_positive(p); best=p; score=score_array(best)
   n=p.size
   # limits by time
   variants=[]
   # reverse mix
   for alpha in (0.3,0.5,0.7): variants.append((alpha*p + (1-alpha)*p[::-1]))
   # power transform
   eps=max(p.max()*1e-12,1e-18)
   for gamma in (0.7,0.85,1.15,1.35): variants.append(np.maximum(p,eps)**gamma)
   # zero small fractions
   quant = np.quantile(p, [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3]) if p.size>10 else []
   for frac in ...
   # smooth: average with log-interp? small kernel? Could do p_smooth = np.convolve(p, ones(5)/5) but nonneg.
```

   for v in variants:
      if time.monotonic()>time_end: break

      if finite and sc<score: best=q.copy(); score=sc
   return best,score
```

Now propose_candidate code more detailed.

```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    start=time.monotonic()
    total=max(1.0,float(budget_s))
    reserve= min(30.0, total*0.02) if total>100 else min(2.0,total*0.1)
    work_deadline = start + max(1.0, total - reserve)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    best_p = None; best_score = np.inf
    # load parent
    try:
       arr = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
       arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
       if arr.size > 0 and np.isfinite(arr).all():
          p=normalize_positive(arr)
          sc=score_array(p)
          if np.isfinite(sc):
              best_p=p.copy(); best_score=sc
    except Exception:
       pass
    if best_p is None:
       # baseline 4096
       n=4096
       p=make_power_series(n)
       best_p=p; best_score=score_array(p)
    # if best too large, for optimization use resampled but keep global best
    MAX_OPT_N=16384
    opt_p=best_p
    if opt_p.size > MAX_OPT_N:
       opt_p=resample_probability(opt_p, MAX_OPT_N)
    # If parent length small, perhaps also create power series alternative and update if better?
    alt_n=opt_p.size
    if alt_n <= 0: alt_n=4096
    alt=make_power_series(alt_n)
    scalt=score_array(alt)
    if scalt < best_score and alt.size <= MAX_OPT_N:
       best_p=alt; best_score=scalt; opt_p=alt
    # noisy variant maybe
    if opt_p.size <= 8192:
       noisy=normalize_positive(opt_p*np.exp(0.15*rng.standard_normal(opt_p.size)))
       scn=score_array(noisy); if scn<best_score: best_p=noisy; best_score=scn; opt_p=noisy
    print(...)

    # Phase 1 target
    if time.monotonic() < work_deadline:
       t1 = min(work_deadline, time.monotonic() + 0.35*(work_deadline-start))
       if opt_p.size <= 10000:
          sc, pp = target_anneal(opt_p, t1, rng, 0.85, "target1")
          if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
              best_score=sc; best_p=pp.copy()
          # if target on downsampled larger than opt? If global best length maybe >opt and remains.
       else:
          sc, pp = target_anneal(opt_p, t1, rng, 0.7, "target1-l")
       opt_p = best_p if best_p.size <= MAX_OPT_N else resample_probability(best_p, MAX_OPT_N)
```

```
def choose_up_len(cur_len):
    sizes=[4096,8192,12288,16384]
    target = int(min(16384, max(4096, cur_len * 1.25)))
    for s in sizes:
       if target <= s:
          if s > cur_len: return s
          return cur_len if cur_len <= 16384 else 16384
    return cur_len
```

Now, code for `postprocess_candidate` no lambda. Let's write:
```
def postprocess_candidate(p, time_end, rng):
    p=normalize_positive(p)
    best=p.copy(); best_score=score_array(best)
    n=p.size
    variants=[]
    if n > 1:
       rev=p[::-1]
       for alpha in [0.3,0.5,0.7]: variants.append(alpha*p+(1-alpha)*rev)
       eps = max(float(p.max())*1e-12, 1e-18)
       for gamma in [0.75,0.85,1.15,1.3]:
           variants.append(np.power(np.maximum(p,eps), gamma))
       # zero small
       if n >= 50:
          for frac in [0.05,0.1,0.2]:
               thr=float(np.quantile(p, frac))
               variants.append(np.where(p > thr, p, 0.0))
       # smoothing: small boxcar? Could alter endpoints. Use np.convolve but normalize.
       if n>=20:
           k=min(5,n//5)
           if k>1:
              kernel=np.ones(k)/k
              sm=np.convolve(p,kernel,mode='same')
              variants.append(sm)
    for v in variants:
       if time.monotonic() > time_end: break
       q=normalize_positive(v)
       sc=score_array(q)
       if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
           best=q.copy(); best_score=sc
    return best,best_score
```
`np.convolve` direct for n=16384 and k=5 direct O(n*k) okay; but in postprocess maybe; evaluator uses direct. OK.

But `np.power` for p values small and gamma>1 may underflow to 0, normalize still okay if some zeros. Good.

Now, final evaluation and fallback:
```
    out = np.clip(best_p, 0.0, 1000.0)
    S=out.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(S) or S <= 0.01:
        # fallback power series 2048
        out = make_power_series(2048)
        S=1.0
    else:
        out = out / S  # scale to sum 1
        if out.sum() < 0.01:
            out = np.ones_like(out)/out.size
    # if out length > 20000, resample? if final large
    if out.size > 20000:
       out = resample_probability(out, 16384)
    # Final evaluate if time
    val=None
    if time.monotonic() < start + max(1,float(budget_s)) - 5 and out.size <= 20000:
       try:
          val=float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in out]))
       except Exception:
          val=np.inf
       if not np.isfinite(val):
           # fallback parent? if have parent original? best maybe.
           val=np.inf
    return [float(x) for x in out]
```

```python
"""
Search for non-negative sequences minimizing

    2 * n * max_k conv(a, a)[k] / (sum(a))**2

The evaluator is scale-invariant, so all local optimization is done on
probability vectors sum(a) = 1.

The program uses a hybrid scheme:
  1. Follow/probabilistically resample the known parent sequence if present.
  2. A flat-target homotopy: from the current best square coefficient vector,
     blend toward a uniform target (or cap peaks), then take a positive
     square root of the target by a stable recurrence.  This explicitly
     searches the manifold near "quadratically flat" polynomials.
  3. Mirror-descent / log-domain refinement with a smooth peak selection
     (soft-max over FFT self-convolution peaks).
  4. Occasional resizing (interpolate log-probability up/down) to exploit
     larger lengths, which usually approach the continuum limit better.
  5. Final post-processing: reversals mixtures, power transforms, support
     pruning, and mild smoothing.

All auxiliary functions are top-level.  The required entry point is
propose_candidate().
"""

import numpy as np
import time


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Basic normalization / scoring helpers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def normalize_positive(a):
    """Return a non-negative 1-D array of floats with sum 1, if possible."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(a.size, 1.0 / float(a.size), dtype=np.float64)
    return a / s


def ensure_positive(a, floor_abs=1e-15, floor_rel=1e-12):
    """
    Normalize to sum 1 and replace exact zeros by a small positive value.
    This is for log-domain optimization.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(a.size, 1.0 / float(a.size), dtype=np.float64)
    a = a / s

    pos = a[a > 0.0]
    if pos.size == 0:
        return np.full(a.size, 1.0 / float(a.size), dtype=np.float64)

    eps = max(floor_abs, floor_rel * float(pos.min()))
    a = np.where(a > 0.0, a, eps)
    a = np.clip(a, eps, 1000.0)
    return a / float(a.sum())


def fast_self_conv(a):
    """Linear self-convolution via FFT."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    m = 2 * n - 1
    size = 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, size)
    return np.fft.irfft(A * A, size)[:m]


def score_array(a):
    """
    Objective value exactly as in the evaluator (scale invariant).
    Returns np.inf if invalid.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.inf
    c = fast_self_conv(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * m / (s * s))


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Smooth peak selection and log-domain gradient
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def softmax_weights(c, beta):
    """
    Stable normalized weights on convolution coefficients.
    beta=0 gives uniform weights; large beta concentrates on the maximum.
    """
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    if c.size == 0:
        return c
    cmax = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(cmax):
        return np.ones(c.shape, dtype=np.float64) / float(c.size)

    z = beta * (c - cmax)
    # Clip only for numerical safety; negative underflow is fine.
    z = np.maximum(z, -50.0)
    with np.errstate(under="ignore", over="ignore"):
        w = np.exp(z)
    s = float(w.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.ones(c.shape, dtype=np.float64) / float(c.size)
    return w / s


def gradient_correlation(p, w):
    """
    Given p (length n, sum 1) and weights w on convolution coefficients
    (length 2n-1), compute

        h_i = 2 * sum_j p_j w[i+j]

    for i=0..n-1.  This is the derivative of sum_k w_k (p*p)_k w.r.t. p_i,
    except for the final factor 2*n used by the score.
    """
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 1:
        return np.array([2.0 * float(p[0]) * float(w[0])], dtype=np.float64)

    L = 2 * n - 1
    if w.size != L:
        w = w[:L]
        if w.size < L:
            w = np.pad(w, (0, L - w.size), mode="constant")

    v = w[::-1]
    full_len = 3 * n - 3
    size = 1 << (full_len - 1).bit_length()

    P = np.fft.rfft(p, size)
    W = np.fft.rfft(v, size)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(P * W, size)[:full_len]

    # h_i = 2 * (p cross-correlated with w)_i
    h = 2.0 * conv[n - 1:2 * n - 1][::-1]
    return h


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Positive square-root by recurrence
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def positive_square_root(b, n):
    """
    Given target coefficients b (length 2n-1), find a non-negative sequence
    a (length n) whose first n convolution coefficients match b as closely
    as possible.  The recurrence solves:

        b_k = sum_{i=0}^k a_i a_{k-i},   k=0..n-1

    starting from a_0=sqrt(b_0).  Negative values are clipped to zero.

    This is a useful map from "flattened square targets" back to sequences.
    """
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    m = b.size
    if m <= 0:
        return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    if n is None or int(n) <= 0:
        n = (m + 1) // 2
    else:
        n = int(n)
        if m != 2 * n - 1:
            n = (m + 1) // 2

    if n <= 1:
        val = float(b[0]) if b.size > 0 else 0.0
        if not np.isfinite(val) or val <= 0.0:
            return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
        return np.array([float(np.sqrt(val))], dtype=np.float64)

    b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)
    b0 = float(b[0])
    if not np.isfinite(b0) or b0 < 1e-12:
        b0 = max(b0, 1e-12) if np.isfinite(b0) else 1e-12

    a = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a0 = float(np.sqrt(b0))
    a[0] = a0

    # conv[k] stores the contribution to coefficient k from previously chosen
    # a_i values only (i.e. the part not containing a_k before it is solved).
    conv = np.zeros(2 * n - 1, dtype=np.float64)
    conv[0] = a0 * a0
    two_a0 = 2.0 * a0

    for k in range(1, n):
        s = conv[k]
        val = (float(b[k]) - s) / two_a0
        if not np.isfinite(val) or val < 0.0:
            val = 0.0
        elif val > 1000.0:
            val = 1000.0

        a[k] = val
        if val != 0.0:
            # Add contributions of a[k] to future coefficients j.
            # For now, only pairs with both indices <= k can be known.
            hi = 2 * k
            if hi > 2 * n - 2:
                hi = 2 * n - 2
            if hi > k:
                seg = hi - k
                conv[k + 1:hi + 1] += (2.0 * val) * a[1:seg + 1]
                if 2 * k <= 2 * n - 2:
                    # The diagonal term a[k]^2 should be counted once,
                    # not twice.
                    conv[2 * k] -= val * val

    return a


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Interpolation / resizing
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def resample_probability(p, N):
    """
    Resize a probability sequence to length N by log-interpolation.
    This is stable for concentrations/singularities.
    """
    p = normalize_positive(p)
    n0 = p.size
    if N <= 0:
        return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    if n0 == N:
        return p.copy()

    if n0 == 1:
        q = np.full(N, 1.0 / float(N), dtype=np.float64)
        return q

    x0 = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n0, dtype=np.float64)
    x1 = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, N, dtype=np.float64)

    eps = 1e-15 / max(n0, 1)
    lp = np.log(np.maximum(p, eps))
    lp1 = np.interp(x1, x0, lp)

    q = np.exp(lp1 - float(np.max(lp1)))
    q = q / float(q.sum())
    return q


def make_power_series(n):
    """
    Coefficients of (1-x)^(-1/2) truncated to length n, normalized.
    This has a well-known good flat-square property:
    its square coefficients are exactly 1 for the first n coefficients.
    """
    if n <= 0:
        return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    c = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    c[0] = 1.0
    for i in range(1, n):
        c[i] = c[i - 1] * (1.0 - 0.5 / float(i))
    return normalize_positive(c)


def choose_up_len(cur_len):
    """A modest enlargement target, capped at 16384."""
    if cur_len <= 0:
        return 4096
    if cur_len > 16384:
        return 16384
    target = int(min(16384, max(4096, cur_len * 1.25)))
    choices = (4096, 8192, 12288, 16384)
    for s in choices:
        if target <= s:
            if s > cur_len:
                return s
            return cur_len
    return cur_len


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Flat-target homotopy
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def target_anneal(p0, time_end, rng, lambda_max=0.85, tag="target"):
    """
    Iteratively take the current best sequence, look at its square
    coefficients, modify them toward flatter/capped targets, and map back
    to a sequence by a positive square-root.  Try left, right, and symmetric
    roots.  Keep any strict improvement.
    """
    p_best = ensure_positive(p0)
    n = p_best.size
    best = score_array(p_best)
    best_p = p_best.copy()

    if not np.isfinite(best):
        return best, best_p

    best_M = best / (2.0 * float(n)) if n > 0 else np.inf
    avg = 1.0 / float(2 * n - 1)

    start = time.monotonic()
    dur = max(1e-6, float(time_end) - start)
    it = 0
    base_b = None
    base_valid = False
    no_improve = 0

    while time.monotonic() < time_end:
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now >= time_end:
            break
        progress = min(1.0, (now - start) / dur)

        if not base_valid:
            base_b = fast_self_conv(best_p)
            base_b = np.maximum(base_b, 0.0)
            base_valid = True

        mode = it % 4
        use_sym = (mode == 1 or mode == 3)
        if use_sym:
            bbase = 0.5 * (base_b + base_b[::-1])
        else:
            bbase = base_b

        if it < 12:
            lam = 0.05 + 0.07 * float(it)
        else:
            lam = float(rng.uniform(0.20, lambda_max))
        lam = min(lambda_max, max(0.02, lam))

        if mode <= 1:
            target = (1.0 - lam) * bbase + lam * avg
        else:
            if np.isfinite(best_M) and best_M > 0.0:
                cap = best_M * (0.65 + 0.55 * float(rng.random()))
                if progress > 0.3:
                    cap *= (1.0 - 0.20 * progress)
                bwork = np.minimum(bbase, cap)
            else:
                bwork = bbase.copy()

            target = 0.90 * bwork + 0.10 * avg
            if mode == 3:
                # Additional pull toward the average on capped targets.
                target = (1.0 - 0.5 * lam) * target + (0.5 * lam) * avg

        ts = float(target.sum())
        if not np.isfinite(ts) or ts <= 0.0:
            target = np.full(2 * n - 1, avg, dtype=np.float64)
            ts = 1.0
        target = target / ts

        # How many roots to compute depends on length/time.
        if n <= 6000:
            do_right = True
            do_sym = True
        elif n <= 10000:
            do_right = True
            do_sym = (it % 3 == 0)
        else:
            do_right = (it % 2 == 1)
            do_sym = False

        cand_score = np.inf
        cand_p = None

        qL = positive_square_root(target, n)
        qL = normalize_positive(qL)
        scL = score_array(qL)
        if np.isfinite(scL):
            cand_score = scL
            cand_p = qL

        if do_right:
            qR = positive_square_root(target[::-1], n)
            qR = qR[::-1]
            qR = normalize_positive(qR)
            scR = score_array(qR)
            if np.isfinite(scR) and scR < cand_score:
                cand_score = scR
                cand_p = qR

        if do_sym and cand_p is not None:
            # Symmetric blend of left and right roots.
            qSym_base = positive_square_root(target[::-1], n)
            qSym_base = qSym_base[::-1]
            qSym = 0.5 * (qL + qSym_base)
            qSym = normalize_positive(qSym)
            scS = score_array(qSym)
            if np.isfinite(scS) and scS < cand_score:
                cand_score = scS
                cand_p = qSym

        if cand_p is not None and np.isfinite(cand_score):
            if cand_score < best:
                best = cand_score
                best_p = cand_p.copy()
                best_M = best / (2.0 * float(n))
                base_valid = False
                no_improve = 0
            else:
                # Small blend with current best keeps the homotopy connected.
                if cand_score < best * 1.05:
                    alpha = 0.10
                else:
                    alpha = 0.03
                blend = (1.0 - alpha) * best_p + alpha * cand_p
                blend = normalize_positive(blend)
                scb = score_array(blend)
                if np.isfinite(scb) and scb < best:
                    best = scb
                    best_p = blend.copy()
                    best_M = best / (2.0 * float(n))
                    base_valid = False
                    no_improve = 0
                else:
                    no_improve += 1
                    if no_improve > 35:
                        no_improve = 0

            if it % 25 == 0:
                print("%s it=%d best=%.7f n=%d" % (tag, it, best, n))

        it += 1

    return best, best_p


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Log-domain mirror-descent refinement
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def mirror_optimize(p0, time_end, rng, beta0=None, beta1=None,
                    lr0=0.16, tag="mirror"):
    """
    Smooth minimization in log(p) space.  p is kept as a probability vector.

    For a soft selection of convolution peaks with weights w,
    let D_i = derivative of 2*n*sum_k w_k (p*p)_k w.r.t. p_i.
    Then d/d log(p_i) is p_i*(D_i - sum_j p_j D_j).  We use Adam-like
    normalization on the projected D to obtain stable multiplicative updates.
    """
    p = ensure_positive(p0)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf, np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)

    if beta0 is None:
        beta0 = max(10.0, 0.20 * float(n))
    if beta1 is None:
        beta1 = max(100.0, 12.0 * float(n))

    best = score_array(p)
    best_p = p.copy()
    if not np.isfinite(best):
        return best, best_p

    u = np.log(p)
    u -= float(np.mean(u))

    m = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    t = 0
    no_improve = 0
    it = 0

    start = time.monotonic()
    dur = max(1e-6, float(time_end) - start)

    while time.monotonic() < time_end:
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now >= time_end:
            break

        progress = min(1.0, (now - start) / dur)
        beta = float(beta0 + (beta1 - beta0) * progress)
        lr = float(lr0 * (1.0 - 0.70 * progress) + 0.005)

        c = fast_self_conv(p)
        s = float(p.sum())
        mcoef = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
        if s <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(mcoef):
            score = np.inf
        else:
            score = float(2.0 * n * mcoef / (s * s))

        if np.isfinite(score) and score < best:
            best = score
            best_p = p.copy()
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            no_improve += 1

        if no_improve > 800:
            # Restart from the best incumbent and inject multiplicative noise.
            p = ensure_positive(best_p)
            noise = float(rng.standard_normal(n))
            p = p * np.exp(0.12 * noise)
            p = normalize_positive(p)
            u = np.log(p)
            u -= float(np.mean(u))
            m[:] = 0.0
            v[:] = 0.0
            t = 0
            no_improve = 0

        if time.monotonic() >= time_end:
            break

        w = softmax_weights(c, beta)
        h = gradient_correlation(p, w)
        D = 2.0 * float(n) * h

        meanD = float(np.dot(p, D))
        g = D - meanD

        rms = float(np.sqrt(np.mean(g * g)))
        if not np.isfinite(rms) or rms <= 1e-14:
            # No useful gradient; add a little noise and continue.
            u += 0.02 * float(rng.standard_normal(n))
            u -= float(np.mean(u))
            p = np.exp(u - float(np.max(u)))
            p /= float(p.sum())
            it += 1
            continue

        z = g / rms
        t += 1
        m = 0.90 * m + 0.10 * z
        v = 0.999 * v + 0.001 * z * z
        step = lr * (m / (np.sqrt(v) + 1e-8))
        step = np.clip(step, -2.0, 2.0)

        u = u - step

        if no_improve > 300 and (it % 5 == 0):
            u += 0.03 * float(rng.standard_normal(n))

        # Center and clip in log-space to avoid underflow.
        u -= float(np.mean(u))
        mx = float(np.max(u))
        u = np.clip(u, mx - 45.0, mx)
        p = np.exp(u - mx)
        ps = float(p.sum())
        if not np.isfinite(ps) or ps <= 0.0:
            p = best_p.copy()
        else:
            p = p / ps
            p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
            ps = float(p.sum())
            if ps <= 0.0:
                p = best_p.copy()
            else:
                p = p / ps

        it += 1
        if it % 150 == 0:
            print("%s iter=%d best=%.7f" % (tag, it, best))

    return best, best_p


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Final post-processing
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def postprocess_candidate(p, time_end, rng):
    """
    Try cheap transforms that often improve near-optimal sequences:
      - mixtures with the reversed sequence,
      - power transforms,
      - pruning very small entries,
      - mild smoothing.
    """
    p = normalize_positive(p)
    best = p.copy()
    best_score = score_array(best)

    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return best, best_score

    variants = []

    if n > 1:
        rev = p[::-1]
        for alpha in (0.30, 0.50, 0.70):
            variants.append(alpha * p + (1.0 - alpha) * rev)

        pmax = float(np.max(p))
        eps = max(1e-18, pmax * 1e-12)
        for gamma in (0.75, 0.85, 1.15, 1.30):
            variants.append(np.power(np.maximum(p, eps), gamma))

        if n >= 50:
            for frac in (0.05, 0.10, 0.20):
                thr = float(np.quantile(p, frac))
                variants.append(np.where(p > thr, p, 0.0))

        if n >= 20:
            k = min(5, max(1, n // 10))
            if k > 1:
                kernel = np.ones(k, dtype=np.float64) / float(k)
                sm = np.convolve(p, kernel, mode="same")
                variants.append(sm)

    for var in variants:
        if time.monotonic() >= time_end:
            break
        q = normalize_positive(var)
        sc = score_array(q)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best = q.copy()
            best_score = sc

    return best, best_score


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Main entry point
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Search for the best non-negative sequence under the 2400-second budget.
    Returns a Python list of floats.
    """
    start = time.monotonic()
    total_budget = max(1.0, float(budget_s))

    # Leave room for a final safety evaluation.  For small budgets, shrink.
    if total_budget > 120.0:
        reserve = 30.0
    elif total_budget > 30.0:
        reserve = 5.0
    else:
        reserve = 1.0
    work_deadline = start + max(1.0, total_budget - reserve)

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    best_p = None
    best_score = np.inf
    MAX_OPT_N = 16384
    TARGET_MAX1 = 10000
    TARGET_MAX2 = 12288

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Initialize from the known parent if available.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
        if arr.size > 0 and np.all(np.isfinite(arr)):
            p = normalize_positive(arr)
            sc = score_array(p)
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                best_p = p.copy()
                best_score = sc
    except Exception:
        pass

    if best_p is None:
        # Robust fallback: truncated (1-x)^(-1/2) coefficients.
        p = make_power_series(4096)
        best_p = p.copy()
        best_score = score_array(p)

    def _work_from_best():
        if best_p is None:
            return make_power_series(4096)
        if best_p.size <= MAX_OPT_N:
            return best_p.copy()
        return resample_probability(best_p, MAX_OPT_N)

    work_p = _work_from_best()

    # Quick diverse alternatives at the working length.
    alt = make_power_series(work_p.size)
    sc_alt = score_array(alt)
    if np.isfinite(sc_alt) and sc_alt < best_score:
        best_score = sc_alt
        best_p = alt.copy()
        work_p = _work_from_best()

    if work_p.size <= 8192:
        noisy = work_p * np.exp(0.15 * float(rng.standard_normal(work_p.size)))
        noisy = normalize_positive(noisy)
        sc_noisy = score_array(noisy)
        if np.isfinite(sc_noisy) and sc_noisy < best_score:
            best_score = sc_noisy
            best_p = noisy.copy()
            work_p = _work_from_best()

    print("initial best_score=%.7f len=%d" % (best_score, best_p.size))

    total_work = max(1.0, float(work_deadline - start))
    end1 = start + 0.35 * total_work
    end2 = start + 0.60 * total_work
    end3 = start + 0.78 * total_work
    end4 = start + 0.90 * total_work
    end5 = start + 0.96 * total_work

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Phase 1: flat-target homotopy on the current sequence.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    if time.monotonic() + 20.0 < min(end1, work_deadline):
        ph_end1 = min(end1, work_deadline)
        if work_p.size <= TARGET_MAX1:
            sc, pp = target_anneal(work_p, ph_end1, rng,
                                   lambda_max=0.85, tag="target1")
        else:
            sc, pp = target_anneal(work_p, ph_end1, rng,
                                   lambda_max=0.70, tag="target1")
        if np.isfinite(sc) and (not np.isfinite(best_score) or sc < best_score):
            best_score = sc
            best_p = pp.copy()
            work_p = _work_from_best()
        print("after phase1 best=%.7f len=%d" % (best_score, best_p.size))
    else:
        # If target is too slow, spend this time on mirror refinement.
        ph_end1 = min(end1, work_deadline)
        if time.monotonic() + 20.0 < ph_end1:
            sc, pp = mirror_optimize(work_p, ph_end1, rng, tag="mirror-pre")
            if np.isfinite(sc) and (not np.isfinite(best_score) or sc < best_score):
                best_score = sc
                best_p = pp.copy()
                work_p = _work_from_best()

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Phase 2: mirror refinement.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    work_p = _work_from_best()
    if time.monotonic() + 20.0 < min(end2, work_deadline):
        sc, pp = mirror_optimize(work_p, min(end2, work_deadline), rng,
                                 tag="mirror2")
        if np.isfinite(sc) and (not np.isfinite(best_score) or sc < best_score):
            best_score = sc
            best_p = pp.copy()
        print("after phase2 best=%.7f len=%d" % (best_score, best_p.size))

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Phase 3: try a larger length (continuum limit is usually helpful).
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    cur_len = best_p.size if best_p.size <= MAX_OPT_N else MAX_OPT_N
    up_len = choose_up_len(cur_len)

    if up_len != cur_len and time.monotonic() + 30.0 < min(end3, work_deadline):
        if best_p.size <= MAX_OPT_N:
            base_for_up = best_p
        else:
            base_for_up = resample_probability(best_p, MAX_OPT_N)
        up_p = resample_probability(base_for_up, up_len)

        ph_end3 = min(end3, work_deadline)
        rem3 = max(0.0, float(ph_end3 - time.monotonic()))
        if up_len <= TARGET_MAX2:
            t_target = time.monotonic() + 0.45 * rem3
            sc, pp = target_anneal(up_p, t_target, rng,
                                   lambda_max=0.80, tag="target-up")
            if np.isfinite(sc) and (not np.isfinite(best_score) or sc < best_score):
                best_score = sc
                best_p = pp.copy()
                work_p = _work_from_best()
        if time.monotonic() + 15.0 < ph_end3:
            if best_p.size <= MAX_OPT_N:
                up_p = resample_probability(best_p, up_len)
            else:
                up_p = resample_probability(resample_probability(best_p, MAX_OPT_N), up_len)
            sc, pp = mirror_optimize(up_p, ph_end3, rng, tag="mirror-up")
            if np.isfinite(sc) and (not np.isfinite(best_score) or sc < best_score):
                best_score = sc
                best_p = pp.copy()
        print("after phase3 best=%.7f len=%d" % (best_score, best_p.size))

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Phase 4: final mirror polish at the best current length.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    work_p = _work_from_best()
    if time.monotonic() + 15.0 < min(end4, work_deadline):
        sc, pp = mirror_optimize(work_p, min(end4, work_deadline), rng,
                                 lr0=0.08, tag="mirror-final")
        if np.isfinite(sc) and (not np.isfinite(best_score) or sc < best_score):
            best_score = sc
            best_p = pp.copy()

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Phase 5: post-processing.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    if time.monotonic() + 5.0 < min(end5, work_deadline):
        pp, sc = postprocess_candidate(
            best_p, min(end5, work_deadline), rng
        )
        if np.isfinite(sc) and (not np.isfinite(best_score) or sc < best_score):
            best_score = sc
            best_p = pp.copy()

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Final safety and output.
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    out = np.asarray(best_p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)

    # Avoid absurdly long outputs for the evaluator's direct convolution.
    if out.size > 20000:
        out = resample_probability(out, 16384)

    s = float(out.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        out = make_power_series(min(4096, max(64, out.size)))
        s = float(out.sum())
    if s <= 0.01:
        out = out / max(s, 1e-300)
        s = float(out.sum())
    if s <= 0.01:
        out = np.ones(out.size if out.size > 0 else 64, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        out = out / s

    # Optional internal verification if there is comfortable time left.
    if out.size <= 20000:
        remaining = (start + total_budget) - time.monotonic()
        if remaining > 12.0:
            try:
                val = float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in out]))
                if np.isfinite(val):
                    print("internal best=%.7f verified=%.7f len=%d time=%.1fs" %
                          (best_score, val, out.size, time.monotonic() - start))
                else:
                    print("internal best=%.7f verified=inf len=%d time=%.1fs" %
                          (best_score, out.size, time.monotonic() - start))
            except Exception:
                print("final evaluation skipped")

        # If verification failed catastrophically and we still have time,
        # fall back to a robust generated sequence.
        try:
            val2 = float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in out]))
            if (not np.isfinite(val2)) or val2 > 2.0:
                out = make_power_series(min(4096, max(64, out.size)))
        except Exception:
            pass

    return [float(x) for x in out]
```
